气候塑造非洲蚊媒疾病传播:对气候决定因素、生物反应和传播动态的综述

Climate shapes mosquito-borne disease transmission in africa: a scoping review of climatic determinants, biological responses and transmission dynamics
作者： Emanuel Catumbela, Wison Nelson Manuel Gonga, Semedo Faria
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景 蚊媒疾病仍然是非洲公共卫生的主要挑战,其传播受气候条件的强烈影响。然而,现有证据在地理分布和方法学上存在不均衡和异质性。目的 映射和综合非洲蚊媒疾病传播受温度、降雨/降水量和相对湿度影响的证据,并比较主要媒介-病原体系统中的气候模式。方法 采用PRISMA-ScR框架进行综述。纳入标准为在非洲进行或提供可识别非洲特异性证据的同行评审原始研究,这些研究调查了疟疾、登革热、基孔肯雅热或寨卡病毒,并评估了温度、降雨/降水量和/或相对湿度与生物、病媒、生态、传播相关或流行病学结果的关系。结果 共纳入18项原始研究:10项关于疟疾,6项关于登革热,2项关于寨卡病毒;没有专门针对基孔肯雅热的研究。温度是最常评估的暴露因素。疟疾研究显示温度与传播之间的非线性且情境依赖关系,包括在接近25 °C时疟疾阳性率出现峰值。登革热研究显示温度信号的异质性,包括在约21 °C以上时温度与发病率之间的关系更强,以及在特定情境下29–30 °C时传播相关结果增加。降雨通常会增加媒介的丰度或疟疾传播,但过量降雨有时会降低幼虫存活率或登革热传播。相对湿度表现出情境依赖效应,包括Aedes aegypti存活与寨卡病毒感染之间的权衡。报告的气候滞后时间主要在1至4个月之间。结论 非洲蚊媒疾病传播的气候影响是非线性的、疾病特异的和情境依赖的。需要更强的气候导向监测和本地化的整合研究。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述聚焦于映射和综合温度、降雨/降水量和相对湿度对非洲蚊媒疾病传播的影响证据,特别强调登革病毒。其目标是比较主要媒介-病原体系统中的气候模式,尤其是涉及登革病毒的系统。
证据基础与方法
采用PRISMA-ScR框架进行了综述。证据基础包括在非洲进行或提供可识别非洲特异性证据的同行评审原始研究,这些研究调查了疟疾、登革热、基孔肯雅热或寨卡病毒,并评估了温度、降雨/降水量和/或相对湿度与生物、病媒、生态、传播相关或流行病学结果的关系。
共识与结论
登革热研究显示温度信号的异质性,包括在约21 °C以上时温度与发病率之间的关系更强,以及在特定情境下29–30 °C时传播相关结果增加。降雨通常会增加媒介的丰度或疟疾传播,但过量降雨有时会降低幼虫存活率或登革热传播。相对湿度表现出情境依赖效应,包括Aedes aegypti存活与寨卡病毒感染之间的权衡。报告的气候滞后时间主要在1至4个月之间。
争议与缺口
现有证据在地理分布和方法学上存在不均衡和异质性。综述强调需要更强的气候导向监测和本地化的整合研究,表明当前证据可能不足以充分理解气候与登革热传播之间的复杂相互作用。
科研与实践启示
需要更强的气候导向监测和本地化的整合研究,以更好地理解和应对气候因素对非洲登革病毒 传播的影响。

Climate shapes mosquito-borne disease transmission in africa: a scoping review of climatic determinants, biological responses and transmission dynamics

Authors: Emanuel Catumbela, Wison Nelson Manuel Gonga, Semedo Faria
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Mosquito-borne diseases remain a major public-health challenge in Africa, and their transmission is strongly influenced by climatic conditions. However, the evidence is geographically uneven and methodologically heterogeneous. Objective To map and synthesise evidence on the influence of temperature, rainfall/precipitation and relative humidity on mosquito-borne disease transmission in Africa, and to compare climatic patterns across major vector–pathogen systems. Methods A scoping review was conducted following PRISMA-ScR. Peer-reviewed primary studies conducted in Africa, or providing identifiable Africa-specific evidence, were included if they investigated malaria, dengue, chikungunya or Zika and assessed temperature, rainfall/precipitation and/or relative humidity in relation to biological, entomological, ecological, transmission-related or epidemiological outcomes. Results Eighteen primary studies were included: 10 on malaria, 6 on dengue and 2 on Zika; none specifically addressed chikungunya. Temperature was the most frequently assessed exposure. Malaria studies showed non-linear and context-dependent temperature relationships, including a field peak in malaria positivity near 25 °C. Dengue studies showed heterogeneous temperature signals, including stronger temperature–incidence relationships above approximately 21 °C and increased transmission-related outcomes around 29–30 °C in specific settings. Rainfall generally increased vector abundance or malaria transmission, although excessive rainfall sometimes reduced larval survival or dengue transmission. Relative humidity showed context-dependent effects, including trade-offs between Aedes aegypti survival and Zika virus infection. Reported climatic lags ranged mainly from one to four months. Conclusion Climatic influences on mosquito-borne disease transmission in Africa are non-linear, disease-specific and context-dependent. Stronger climate-informed surveillance and locally resolved integrated research are needed.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on mapping and synthesising evidence on the influence of temperature, rainfall/precipitation and relative humidity on mosquito-borne disease transmission in Africa, with a specific emphasis on dengue virus. It aims to compare climatic patterns across major vector–pathogen systems, particularly those involving dengue virus.
Evidence base and method
A scoping review was conducted following PRISMA-ScR. The evidence base includes peer-reviewed primary studies conducted in Africa, or providing identifiable Africa-specific evidence, that investigated malaria, dengue, chikungunya or Zika and assessed temperature, rainfall/precipitation and/or relative humidity in relation to biological, entomological, ecological, transmission-related or epidemiological outcomes.
Consensus and conclusions
Dengue studies showed heterogeneous temperature signals, including stronger temperature–incidence relationships above approximately 21 °C and increased transmission-related outcomes around 29–30 °C in specific settings. Rainfall generally increased vector abundance or malaria transmission, although excessive rainfall sometimes reduced larval survival or dengue transmission. Relative humidity showed context-dependent effects, including trade-offs between Aedes aegypti survival and Zika virus infection. Reported climatic lags ranged mainly from one to four months.
Controversies and gaps
The evidence is geographically uneven and methodologically heterogeneous. The review highlights the need for stronger climate-informed surveillance and locally resolved integrated research, indicating that current evidence may not be sufficient to fully understand the complex interactions between climate and dengue transmission.
Research and practice implications
Stronger climate-informed surveillance and locally resolved integrated research are needed to better understand and address the impact of climatic factors on dengue virus transmission in Africa.

新闻简报瓦努阿图登革热暴发情况报告#16,发布于2026年11月17日,详细描述了埃法特岛、桑托岛、塔纳岛和马莱库拉岛当前的登革热疫情情况。报告中将疑似病例定义为患有急性发热性疾病超过两天,并出现两个或更多症状(如食欲不振、恶心、疼痛、皮疹、白细胞减少、止血带试验阳性或警告迹象)的个体。确诊病例需要通过分离登革病毒或检测登革热特异性抗原或抗体进行实验室确认。共提交了16份样本进行确认性检测和血清型鉴定,所有16份样本在埃法特岛和桑托岛均被确认为登革病毒类型1(DENV-1)。然而,塔纳岛的血清型仍待确认。暴发的阈值(即一个流行病学周内报告4例确诊病例)已被满足,表明存在重大的公共卫生问题。报告未提供关于调查、检测、隔离或接触追踪措施的具体细节,塔纳岛的血清型状态仍不确定。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告发布于2026年11月17日,暴发的阈值被定义为在一个流行病学周内报告4例确诊病例。
地点与对象
该报告涵盖瓦努阿图的埃法特岛、桑托岛、塔纳岛和马莱库拉岛,重点关注这些地区登革病毒的活动情况。
事件
报告了一起登革热暴发疫情,疑似病例定义为急性发热性疾病持续超过2天并符合特定临床标准的个体,确诊病例则需要通过实验室检测登革病毒或特异性抗原/抗体来确认。
规模、影响与风险
共提交了16份样本进行确认性检测和血清型鉴定,所有16份样本在埃法特岛和桑托岛均被确认为登革病毒类型1(DENV-1)。塔纳岛的血清型仍待确认。暴发的阈值已被满足,表明存在重大的公共卫生问题。
应对、状态与不确定性
该报告概述了暴发的阈值和病例定义,但未提供关于调查、检测、隔离或接触追踪措施的具体细节。塔纳岛的血清型状态仍不确定。

Vanuatu Dengue Outbreak Situation Report #16 (Efate, Santo, Tanna and Malekula Island) - ReliefWeb

News Brief
Vanuatu's Dengue Outbreak Situation Report #16, published on 17 November 2026, details the ongoing dengue situation on Efate, Santo, Tanna, and Malekula Islands. The report defines suspected cases as individuals with acute fever illness lasting more than two days and presenting two or more symptoms such as anorexia, nausea, aches, rash, low white blood cell count, tourniquet test positivity, or warning signs. Confirmed cases require laboratory confirmation through the isolation of dengue virus or detection of dengue-specific antigens or antibodies. Sixteen samples were referred for testing and serotyping, with all 16 samples confirmed as Dengue Virus Type 1 (DENV-1) on Efate and Santo Island. However, the serotype for Tanna Island is still pending. The outbreak threshold of four confirmed cases within an epidemiological week has been met, indicating a significant public health concern. The report does not provide specific details on investigation, testing, isolation, or contact tracing measures, and the status of Tanna Island remains unresolved.
Original source text (English)

Outbreak threshold: Four confirmed dengue cases reported within a single epidemiological week. Circulating serotype: 16 samples were referred for confirmatory testing and serotyping. All 16 samples were confirmed as Dengue Virus Type 1 (DENV-1) for both Efate and Santo Island. Tanna Island serotype still pending. View five news elements
Time
The report was published on 17 Nov 2026, and the outbreak threshold was defined as four confirmed dengue cases reported within a single epidemiological week.
Location and population
The report covers Efate, Santo, Tanna, and Malekula Islands in Vanuatu, with a focus on dengue virus activity in these regions.
Event
A dengue outbreak was reported, with suspected cases defined by acute fever illness lasting more than 2 days and meeting specific clinical criteria, and confirmed cases requiring laboratory testing for dengue virus or specific antigens/antibodies.
Scale, impact and risk
Sixteen samples were referred for confirmatory testing and serotyping, with all 16 samples confirmed as Dengue Virus Type 1 (DENV-1) on Efate and Santo Island. The Tanna Island serotype is still pending. The outbreak threshold was met, indicating a significant public health concern.
Response, status and uncertainty
The report outlines the outbreak threshold and case definitions, but no specific details on investigation, testing, isolation, or contact tracing are provided. The status of Tanna Island's serotype remains unresolved.

美洲地区登革热流行病学情况 - 流行病学第42周,2026年 - ReliefWeb

Dengue Epidemiological Situation in the Region of the Americas - Epidemiological Week 42, 2026 - ReliefWeb
新闻简报The ReliefWeb report from 14 November 2026 details the dengue epidemiological situation in the Region of the Americas for epidemiological week 42, 2026. It reports a total of 1,914,591 suspected dengue cases, with a cumulative incidence of 191 cases per 100,000 population. This represents a 53% decrease compared to the same period in 2025 and a 61% decrease compared to the average of the last five years. Out of these cases, 605,321 were laboratory-confirmed, and 4,059 were classified as severe dengue, resulting in 655 deaths and a case fatality rate of 0.034%. The report highlights that 22 countries and territories reported dengue cases, with some subregions showing significant changes in case trends. For instance, Central America and Mexico saw a 35% decrease compared to the same week in 2025, while Costa Rica and Mexico experienced increases of 41% and 39%, respectively, compared to the previous four weeks. The report serves as an official update and analysis by PAHO, providing insights into the current situation and trends.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告涵盖2026年第42流行病学周,发布日期为2026年11月14日。
地点与对象
The Region of the Americas, including 22 countries and territories, with a cumulative incidence of 191 cases per 100,000 population.
事件
2026年第42流行病学周,美洲地区共报告了1,914,591例疑似登革热病例,由PAHO发布。
规模、影响与风险
在1,914,591例疑似病例中,605,321例为实验室确诊,4,059例被归类为重症登革热。共记录了655例死亡,病死率为0.034%。中美洲和墨西哥与2025年同期相比下降了35%,与过去五年平均值相比下降了64%。
应对、状态与不确定性
该报告提供了按次区域的流行病学更新与分析,展示了相关数据和趋势。数据基于报告的病例,并与往年数据和平均值进行了比较。

2026年11月17日太平洋地区疫情及新发疾病警报 - ReliefWeb

Epidemic and emerging disease alerts in the Pacific as of 17 November 2026 - ReliefWeb
新闻简报The ReliefWeb report from 17 November 2026 highlights dengue outbreaks in the Pacific region, particularly in the Marshall Islands, Samoa, and Vanuatu. In the Marshall Islands, the 20th Outbreak Situation Report records 173 positive cases (confirmed and probable) among 615 specimens tested, with the last confirmed case on Majuro having onset on 18 October. In Vanuatu, Situation Report #16 reports 259 new laboratory-confirmed cases from EpiWeek 41 to 45, mostly on Tanna and Santo, with a cumulative total of 476 confirmed cases across four islands. The positivity rate is 26% in Majuro and 35% in Ebeye, with attack rates of 5.5 and 4.6 per 1,000 population, respectively. DENV-1 accounts for 52% of serotyped cases, and DENV-2 for 48%. No deaths or severe dengue cases were reported in Vanuatu. The red alert for dengue remains in effect for Vanuatu, while the blue alert is in place for Marshall Islands. The outbreak in Vanuatu is ongoing, with no sustained decrease in cases yet. The report also notes a measles case in Australia and a pertussis outbreak in Vanuatu, but these are not the primary focus. The Ministry of Health in Vanuatu continues to monitor the situation closely and may declare the outbreak over after 42 days without new cases.
查看新闻五要素
时间
The report was published on 17 November 2026. The outbreak data spans from 15 August to 12 November 2026 for Marshall Islands, and from 05 October to 08 November 2026 for Vanuatu. The most recent cases in Majuro had onset on 18 October, and in Ebeye on 09 November.
地点与对象
该报告涵盖马绍尔群岛(马朱罗和埃贝岛)、萨摩亚、瓦努阿图(埃法特、桑托、坦纳和马莱库拉)以及密克罗尼西亚联邦(雅普州)。受影响的人群包括15岁以下儿童,他们占登革热病例的73%,以及受影响地区的一般人群。
事件
登革病毒暴发在马绍尔群岛、萨摩亚和瓦努阿图被报告。马绍尔群岛在615份检测样本中发现了173例阳性病例(确认和可能病例),其中最后确认的病例在马朱罗岛发病时间为10月18日。在瓦努阿图,从EpiWeek 41到45期间报告了259例新的实验室确认病例,暴发仍在持续并受到密切监测。报告还提到了澳大利亚的一例麻疹病例和瓦努阿图的一例百日咳暴发,但这些并非主要关注点。
规模、影响与风险
In the Marshall Islands, 173 positive cases were recorded, with 18 hospitalisations (all children) and no deaths. In Vanuatu, 476 confirmed cases were reported across four islands, with 20,553 clinically diagnosed cases and 5,550 laboratory-confirmed cases since 1st January 2025. Positivity rates were 26% in Majuro and 35% in Ebeye, with attack rates of 5.5 and 4.6 per 1,000 population, respectively. DENV-1 accounted for 52% of serotyped cases, and DENV-2 for 48%. No severe dengue or deaths were reported in Vanuatu.
应对、状态与不确定性
马绍尔群岛卫生与人类服务部和瓦努阿图卫生部正在密切监测暴发。瓦努阿图的登革热红色警报仍然有效,而马绍尔群岛的蓝色警报已更新。瓦努阿图的暴发尚未被宣布结束,卫生部可能在42天无新病例后考虑结束该警报。报告还提到法国波利尼西亚正在进行流感疫苗接种活动,并且瓦努阿图对百日咳进行密切监测。

Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA World + 11 more Epidemic and emerging disease alerts in the Pacific as of 17 November 2026 Format Map Source SPC Posted 17 Nov 2026 Originally published 17 Nov 2026 Attachments Download Map (PDF | 549.46 KB) Highlights/updates since the last map was sent on PacNet on 10 November 2026: Dengue • Marshall Islands: The 20th Outbreak Situation Report, covering 15 August to 12 November 2026, records 173 positive cases (confirmed and probable) among 615 specimens tested: Majuro 128 and Ebeye 45, against 171 in the 18th report covering 15 August to 02 November 2026. All new positive cases since 29 October came from Ebeye, the most recent with onset on 09 November, while in Majuro the last confirmed case had onset on 18 October. There have been 18 hospitalisations since the beginning of the outbreak (Majuro 13, Ebeye 5), all children and no deaths. Positivity rates are 26% in Majuro and 35% in Ebeye, and attack rates 5.5 and 4.6 per 1,000 population, respectively. 71% of positive cases are under 20 years of age. Only DENV-1 has been identified. The red alert for dengue (DENV-1) is changed to a blue alert for Marshall Islands. – Source: Republic of the Marshall Islands Ministry of Health and Human Services, Communicable Disease Weekly Report for EpiWeek 45, including the 20th Outbreak Situation Report on dengue fever in Majuro and Ebeye shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 16 November 2026. • Samoa: Dengue Fever Outbreak Situation Report Issue No. 81 covering EpiWeek 44 (26 October to 1st November 2026) reports 37 new cases, up 3% on the previous week, with one laboratory-confirmed case (in comparaison to two in EpiWeek 43), two new admissions and no death. Since 1st January 2025, 20,553 clinically diagnosed cases, 5,550 laboratory-confirmed cases and nine dengue-related deaths have been recorded. DENV-1 accounts for 52% and DENV-2 for 48% of serotyped cases, and children under 15 years for 73% of cases. The blue alert for dengue (DENV-1 & DENV-2) remains in effect. – Source: Ministry of Health Samoa, Dengue Fever Outbreak Situation Report Issue No. 81 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 16 November 2026. • Vanuatu: Situation Report #16 on the dengue outbreak reports 259 new laboratory-confirmed cases from EpiWeek 41 to 45 (05 October to 08 November 2026), mostly on Tanna (152) and Santo (76), with 20 on Efate and 17 on Malekula. The cumulative total reaches 476 confirmed cases on four islands: Efate 178, Tanna 167, Santo 100 and Malekula 18. Weekly cases peaked at 109 in EpiWeek 42, driven by Tanna (73 cases), then fell to 41 in EpiWeek 43 and 22 in each of EpiWeeks 44 and 45. Transmission, first concentrated in Shefa Province, increased sharply in Tafea between EpiWeeks 40 and 42. Seventeen dengue-associated hospitalisations have been reported, all discharged, with no severe dengue and no deaths. DENV-1 has been confirmed in 16 samples from Efate and Santo, and serotyping of Tanna samples is pending. The Ministry of Health keeps the outbreak under close observation until a sustained decrease is seen on all affected islands. The red alert for dengue (DENV-1) remains in effect for Vanuatu. – Source: Vanuatu Ministry of Health, Dengue Outbreak on Efate, Santo, Tanna and Malekula Island, Situation Report #16 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 17 November 2026. Diarrhoea • Federated States of Micronesia – Yap State: The Yap weekly bulletin for EpiWeek 45 (02 to 08 November 2026) shows diarrhoea below the alert threshold for a second consecutive week, with 3 cases against 5 in EpiWeek 44 (alert threshold 7), after ten consecutive weeks on alert. One of four stool specimens tested was positive for adenovirus. The blue alert for diarrhoea remains in effect for the Federated States of Micronesia (Yap State). – Source: Yap State Department of Health Services, Communicable Disease Weekly Bulletin shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 13 November 2026. Measles • Australia: On 12 November 2026, NSW Health reported a confirmed measles case in a traveller recently returned from South Asia, where outbreaks are ongoing. While infectious, the person visited several places in Sydney, including Sydney Kingsford Smith International Airport and a healthcare facility. New South Wales has confirmed 58 measles cases since 1st January 2026. In Queensland, one measles notification was recorded in the week starting 02 November 2026, after none in the three previous weeks. Queensland has recorded 18 measles cases in 2026, 13 acquired overseas and 5 locally. Nationally, the National Notifiable Disease Surveillance System recorded 125 measles notifications in 2026 as of 17 November 2026, against 181 in 2025. The red alert for measles remains in effect for Australia. – Sources: NSW Health, Measles alert for Sydney, Queensland Health, Notifiable conditions weekly totals and summary information and the National Communicable Disease Surveillance Dashboard (NNDSS) accessed on 17 November 2026. Pertussis/Whooping cough • Vanuatu: Situation Report 7 on the pertussis outbreak in Penama Province reports no new cases on Ambae Island from EpiWeek 43 to 46 (26 October to 15 November 2026). The cumulative total remains 634 cases, of which 7 are laboratory confirmed. The last cases were reported in EpiWeek 41 (7 cases), after the peak of EpiWeeks 36 and 37 (266 and 307 cases). As of EpiWeek 46, five consecutive weeks have passed without a new case, which suggests that transmission may have been interrupted. The outbreak has not yet been declared over. The Ministry of Health may consider doing so once 42 days (two maximum incubation periods) have passed since the onset of the last case. Two cases have been hospitalised since the start of the outbreak, both discharged, and no death has been reported. The red alert for pertussis is changed to a blue alert for Vanuatu. – Source: Vanuatu Ministry of Health, Pertussis Outbreak in Penama Province, Situation Report 07 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 17 November 2026. Respiratory diseases • French Polynesia: In EpiWeek 45 (02 to 08 November 2026), French Polynesia remained in an influenza epidemic phase, with an increase to 111 laboratory-confirmed cases, 79 of them influenza A, and 38 hospitalisations, including four admissions to intensive care. No new death was reported. Since 05 October 2026 (EpiWeek 41), 283 confirmed cases (233 influenza A), 93 hospitalisations, seven intensive care admissions and three deaths have been recorded. These figures are still to be consolidated. Consultations for acute respiratory infection in the sentinel network also increased, while their proportion remained stable and more physicians reported than in the two previous weeks. Adenovirus, metapneumovirus, rhino/enterovirus and parainfluenza 4 were also detected. The influenza vaccination campaign is ongoing. The red alert for influenza A remains in effect for French Polynesia. – Source: Agence de régulation de l'action sanitaire et sociale (ARASS), Bulletin de Surveillance Sanitaire de Polynésie française n°40 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 17 November 2026. • New Caledonia: On 09 November 2026, the Department of Health and Social Affairs (DASS) declared the end of the influenza epidemic, all clinical and virological indicators having returned to their usual level, with residual low-level circulation. The A(H1N1) epidemic had been declared on 25 September 2026 (EpiWeek 39). After a peak of 151 confirmed cases in EpiWeek 40, cases fell to 2 in EpiWeek 45, and test positivity from 20% in EpiWeek 44 to 5%. Over EpiWeeks 37 to 45, 599 cases were laboratory-confirmed, and A(H1N1) made up 99.3% of the 420 subtyped samples. People aged 65 and over accounted for 36% of cases. Acute respiratory infections represented 1.8% of emergency department presentations in EpiWeek 45, against a peak of 13% in EpiWeek 41, and 29% of presentations for acute respiratory infection during the epidemic led to hospitalisation. The red alert for influenza A is removed for New Caledonia. – Source: Department of Health and Social Affairs (DASS) of New Caledonia, Vigilance DASS bulletin 2026-#12 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 10 November 2026. • New Zealand: The laboratory-based virology weekly report for week 45 (ending 08 November 2026) records 5,960 influenza-positive specimens since 1st January 2026: 5,514 influenza A (963 A(H1N1)pdm09, 877 A(H3N2) and 3,674 not subtyped) and 446 influenza B. This is 138 more than in the week 43 report, indicating that detections are slowing. The acute respiratory illness dashboard published its last weekly update of the season for the week ending 25 October 2026, and the next update is expected on 26 November 2026. The blue alert for influenza A remains in effect for New Zealand. – Source: PHF Science, New Zealand laboratory-based virology weekly report and Respiratory illness dashboard accessed on 17 November 2026. • Tonga: The weekly syndromic surveillance report for EpiWeek 45 (02 to 08 November 2026) shows influenza-like illness falling for a fourth week, from 54 to 49 cases, while severe acute respiratory infections rose from 3 to 9. From 03 August to 08 November 2026, 623 of 835 ILI cases (74.6%) were tested: 270 (43.3%) were positive for influenza A, 6 for influenza B and 31 for RSV. A total of 101 SARI patients have been hospitalised and a fifth death has occurred among SARI cases, up from four reported in EpiWeek 43. The red alert for influenza A remains in effect for Tonga. – Source: Tonga Ministry of Health, Weekly Communicable Diseases Syndromic Surveillance shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 12 November 2026. • Vanuatu: The first situation report on the influenza A outbreak in Shefa and Tafea provinces, covering EpiWeeks 30 to 45 (20 July to 08 November 2026), reports 114 laboratory-confirmed influenza A cases out of 232 samples tested. Confirmed cases rose from 2 in EpiWeek 41 to a peak of 40 in EpiWeek 43, then fell to 32 in EpiWeek 44 and 12 in EpiWeek 45. Efate accounts for 98 cases (86%) and Tanna for 16 (14%), where the first cases were detected in EpiWeek 44. Subtyping identified influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 as the circulating strain. Thirty-three confirmed cases have been hospitalised and no death has been reported. Influenza-like illness reached 7,315 cases over the period, 5,943 in Shefa and 1,372 in Tafea, with a peak of 981 in EpiWeek 43 followed by 754 and 533 cases, still above the alert threshold. The outbreak, declared in Shefa in EpiWeek 42 and in Tafea in EpiWeek 44, remains ongoing. The red alert for influenza A remains in effect for Vanuatu. – Source: Vanuatu Ministry of Health, Influenza A Outbreak, Shefa and Tafea Province, Situation Report 01 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 17 November 2026. Rotavirus • Vanuatu: The first joint situation report on the rotavirus outbreak in Tafea and Sanma provinces, covering EpiWeeks 30 to 45 (20 July to 08 November 2026), reports 1,202 cases, of which 12 are laboratory confirmed (3 in Tafea and 9 in Sanma). Tanna accounts for 929 clinically diagnosed cases (78%), Santo for 259 (22%) and Malo for 2. Cases peaked at 251 in EpiWeek 37 and have declined steadily since, to 8 in EpiWeek 45, although sporadic transmission continues. Children under five account for 82% of cases. Tafea has reported 46 hospitalisations and one death since the start of the outbreak, and no patient remains in hospital. The blue alert for rotavirus remains in effect for Vanuatu. – Source: Vanuatu Ministry of Health, Rotavirus Outbreak, Tafea and Sanma Province, Situation Report 01 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 17 November 2026. Other Information: Dengue • American Samoa: On 12 November 2026, the US CDC published in its Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report a review of the dengue outbreak in American Samoa. From June 2025 to August 2026, 1,048 confirmed dengue cases were identified (211 per 10,000 population), 95% locally acquired and 49 travel-associated. A total of 371 patients (35%) were hospitalised and none died. Of the confirmed cases, 78% of cases were under 20 years of age, and incidence was highest among children aged 10 to 14 years (652 per 10,000). Of 146 serotyped cases, 93% were DENV-1 and 7% DENV-2. No travel-associated case was identified after 15 February 2026, and case counts declined from February to August 2026. – Source: US CDC, Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report, Notes from the Field: Dengue Outbreak, American Samoa, 2025–2026 accessed on 17 November 2026. Hantavirus • Global: On 12 November 2026, the Institut Pasteur published the results of coordinated genomic sequencing of the Andes hantavirus cluster on the MV Hondius expedition cruise (April to May 2026, 13 cases and three deaths), carried out by laboratories in seven countries. The viruses showed low genetic diversity, consistent with a single zoonotic spillover event followed by human-to-human transmission through close and prolonged contact. Eight of ten apparent mutations were sequencing artefacts, and there was no evidence of airborne spread. – Source: Institut Pasteur, Andes hantavirus: characterization of a rare disease cluster by coordinated genomic sequencing, accessed on 17 November 2026. Respiratory diseases • Northern Mariana Islands: The weekly syndromic surveillance report for EpiWeek 44 (1st to 07 November 2026) records 24 laboratory-confirmed influenza cases (22 influenza A, 2 A(H1N1)pdm09), up from 11 in EpiWeek 43, for 427 in 2026. Influenza-like illness rose to 41 cases, 64% above the average of the three previous weeks. Subtyping by the Guam Public Health Laboratory, released on 22 October 2026, identified three A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses. – Source: Commonwealth of the Northern Mariana Islands, Weekly Syndromic Surveillance Report shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 16 November 2026. Time
The report was published on 17 November 2026. The outbreak data spans from 15 August to 12 November 2026 for Marshall Islands, and from 05 October to 08 November 2026 for Vanuatu. The most recent cases in Majuro had onset on 18 October, and in Ebeye on 09 November.
Location and population
The report covers the Marshall Islands (Majuro and Ebeye), Samoa, Vanuatu (Efate, Santo, Tanna, and Malekula), and the Federated States of Micronesia (Yap State). The affected population includes children under 15 years of age, who account for 73% of dengue cases, and the general population in the affected regions.
Event
Dengue outbreaks were reported in the Marshall Islands, Samoa, and Vanuatu. The Marshall Islands had 173 positive cases (confirmed and probable) among 615 specimens tested, with the last confirmed case on Majuro having onset on 18 October. In Vanuatu, 259 new laboratory-confirmed cases were reported from EpiWeek 41 to 45, with the outbreak ongoing and under close observation. The report also mentions a measles case in Australia and a pertussis outbreak in Vanuatu, but these are not the primary focus.
Scale, impact and risk
In the Marshall Islands, 173 positive cases were recorded, with 18 hospitalisations (all children) and no deaths. In Vanuatu, 476 confirmed cases were reported across four islands, with 20,553 clinically diagnosed cases and 5,550 laboratory-confirmed cases since 1st January 2025. Positivity rates were 26% in Majuro and 35% in Ebeye, with attack rates of 5.5 and 4.6 per 1,000 population, respectively. DENV-1 accounted for 52% of serotyped cases, and DENV-2 for 48%. No severe dengue or deaths were reported in Vanuatu.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Marshall Islands Ministry of Health and Human Services and Vanuatu Ministry of Health are monitoring the outbreaks closely. The red alert for dengue in Vanuatu is still active, and the blue alert for Marshall Islands has been updated. The outbreak in Vanuatu is not yet declared over, and the Ministry of Health may consider ending it after 42 days without new cases. The report also mentions ongoing influenza vaccination campaigns in French Polynesia and close monitoring of pertussis in Vanuatu.

登革热患者病情危重;医生警告病情恶化的迹象。- Vietnam.vn

Dengue fever patient in critical condition; doctor warns of signs of worsening condition. - Vietnam.vn
新闻简报越南新闻网站Vietnam.vn于2026年11月14日发布的一则报道指出,一名登革热患者病情危重,出现了血小板减少、肝损伤和凝血障碍等严重并发症。该患者BH(53岁,海防市)在入院前约5天出现高热及其他症状。国家热带病医院的医生警告称,该疾病即使在退烧后仍可能恶化,尤其是在发病后的第3至第7天之间。另一名患者PL(23岁,河内市)也表现出严重症状,并被诊断为登革热。医院正在治疗16名登革热患者,其中包括重症病例。2026年前10个月,越南记录了超过170,000例登革热病例,与去年同期相比增加了26%,并有21例死亡。南部地区占病例总数的66.8%,其次是中央高地(17.5%)、中部地区(9.8%)和北部地区(5.9%)。报告强调了密切监测、及时治疗以及避免自行使用NSAIDs药物的重要性。同时,报告还指出需要采取积极的预防措施和疫苗接种以应对该疾病。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该新闻报道发布于2026-11-14。患者BH入院前约5天出现症状,患者PL入院前约7天出现症状。该疾病的重症阶段通常出现在发病后的第3至第7天之间。
地点与对象
病例发生在越南的海防市和河内市。受影响的人群包括两名女性患者,年龄分别为23岁和53岁,其中23岁患者没有明显的基础疾病。国家热带病医院正在治疗16名登革热患者,其中包括重症病例。
事件
一名登革热患者病情危重,出现了严重并发症,包括血小板减少、肝损伤和凝血障碍。国家热带病医院的医生警告称,该疾病可能表现出病情恶化的迹象,并存在重症登革热出血热的风险。
规模、影响与风险
2026年前10个月,越南记录了超过170,000例登革热病例,与去年同期相比增加了26%,并有21例死亡。南部地区占病例总数的66.8%,其次是中央高地(17.5%)、中部地区(9.8%)和北部地区(5.9%)。该疾病可能进展为严重阶段,存在血浆渗漏、休克和多器官衰竭的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该患者已被转至国家热带病医院进行进一步的治疗治疗。医生强调,对于重症登革热病例,需要密切监测和强化治疗。卫生部报告了暴发的整体规模,专家建议在适当情况下采取预防措施和疫苗接种。

Dengue fever patient in critical condition; doctor warns of signs of worsening condition. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
A news report from Vietnam.vn on November 14, 2026, highlights a dengue fever patient in critical condition, with severe complications such as low platelet counts, liver damage, and coagulation disorders. The patient, BH (53 years old, Hai Phong), developed high fever and other symptoms approximately 5 days before hospitalization. Doctors at the National Hospital for Tropical Diseases warned that the disease can worsen even when fever subsides, especially between the 3rd and 7th day of onset. Another patient, PL (23 years old, Hanoi), also showed severe symptoms and was diagnosed with dengue fever. The hospital is treating 16 patients, including severe cases. In the first 10 months of 2026, Vietnam recorded over 170,000 dengue cases, a 26% increase compared to the same period, with 21 deaths. The South region accounted for 66.8% of cases. The report emphasizes the importance of close monitoring, timely treatment, and avoiding self-medication with NSAIDs. It also highlights the need for proactive prevention measures and vaccination to combat the disease.
Original source text (English)
English Login Homepage Topic News Political System Destination Event Travel Happy Vietnam Enterprise Product Heritage Museum Figure Multimedia Data Home Culture – Society Medical Dengue fever patient in critical condition; doctor warns of signs of worsening condition. Patients aged 23 and 53 with dengue fever were hospitalized with severely low platelet counts, serious liver damage, and blood clotting disorders. Check Symptoms Báo Đầu tư•14/11/2026 Follow Vietnam.vn on Google Add Vietnam.vn on Google 0 The National Hospital for Tropical Diseases is currently treating several cases of dengue fever, including two female patients aged 23 and 53 who were admitted with severely low platelet counts, liver damage, and warning signs of dangerous complications. The first case involves patient BH (53 years old, Hai Phong ), who was previously completely healthy and had never had Dengue fever. Approximately 5 days before hospitalization, the patient developed a persistent high fever, at times reaching 39-40°C, accompanied by general body aches, nausea, frequent vomiting, and abdominal pain. Doctors warn that the disease can become dangerous even when the patient's fever begins to subside, especially during the period from the 3rd to the 7th day. Despite self-medicating with paracetamol at home, the patient's condition did not improve, so they went to a medical facility for examination and were diagnosed with Dengue virus infection. Test results showed a rapid decrease in platelet count from 38 to 21 G/L, and elevated liver enzymes, forcing doctors to transfer the patient to the National Hospital for Tropical Diseases for further treatment. Check Symptoms Here, the patient still had a high fever, severe pain in the right lower rib area, and petechiae (small hemorrhagic spots) under the skin at the injection site. Notably, tests showed platelet count was only 23 G/L, liver enzyme levels AST was elevated to 1,939 U/L, ALT was 804 U/L, along with a blood clotting disorder. Doctors diagnosed the patient with severe dengue fever, liver damage, and coagulation disorders, requiring close monitoring to detect early risks of bleeding, circulatory problems, and life-threatening complications. Another case involves patient PL (23 years old, Hanoi ), who had no notable underlying medical conditions. Approximately 7 days before being transferred to the hospital, the patient experienced intermittent high fever, sometimes reaching 39°C, accompanied by chills, headache, body aches, abdominal pain, nausea, frequent vomiting, and loose stools. After initial treatment at the medical facility, the patient's condition did not improve, while laboratory test results continued to deteriorate, with platelet count dropping to 21 G/L, liver enzymes AST increasing to 1,907 U/L and ALT to 883 U/L. Find Treatments Specifically, the ultrasound results revealed fluid in the abdominal cavity and bilateral pleural spaces, suggesting plasma leakage, one of the dangerous mechanisms of severe Dengue hemorrhagic fever, which can cause decreased circulating volume, hemodynamic instability, and lead to shock if not treated promptly. Upon transfer to the National Hospital for Tropical Diseases, the patient still had a fever, was very tired, had petechiae (small hemorrhagic spots) under the skin, and experienced pain in the liver area. Given the severely low platelet count, serious liver damage, and warning signs of complications, the doctors determined that the patient had severe Dengue hemorrhagic fever requiring close monitoring and intensive treatment. Currently, the National Hospital for Tropical Diseases is treating 16 patients with dengue fever, including some severe cases requiring continuous monitoring to promptly address complications. According to Dr. Pham Van Phuc, Deputy Director of the Intensive Care Unit at the National Hospital for Tropical Diseases, dengue fever is an infectious disease caused by the dengue virus, primarily transmitted through Aedes mosquitoes, and can occur at all ages, including young people or those who were previously completely healthy. According to Dr. Phuc, thrombocytopenia is a common symptom in dengue fever patients; however, the severity of the disease cannot be assessed solely based on platelet count. A comprehensive assessment of clinical signs, bleeding status, blood pressure, pulse, urine output, blood concentration, and organ damage is necessary. Check Symptoms You may also like In the two recently admitted patients, in addition to severely low platelet counts, they also had very high liver enzyme levels, coagulation disorders, and other warning signs, indicating that the disease had progressed to a serious stage, posing a potential risk of severe bleeding, shock, and multi-organ damage. One of the dangerous characteristics of dengue fever is that the disease can worsen precisely when the patient's fever begins to subside, leading many to become complacent, assuming their health has improved and not continuing close monitoring. Typically, the critical phase occurs between the 3rd and 7th day of the disease's onset, with the risk of plasma leakage, severe bleeding, or damage to organs such as the liver, heart, and nervous system. Find Treatments If plasma leakage progresses rapidly without being detected and treated promptly, the patient may experience decreased circulating volume, hypotension, shock, and multiple organ failure, or even death. Dr. Phuc specifically noted that patients and their families should not assess the severity of dengue fever based solely on body temperature, because the absence or reduction of fever does not necessarily mean the disease has passed the dangerous stage. People with dengue fever should be taken to a medical facility immediately if they experience warning signs such as severe or persistent abdominal pain, frequent vomiting, extreme fatigue, lethargy, bleeding gums, nosebleeds, decreased urination, or any unusual changes in consciousness and circulation. During home monitoring, patients need to rest, drink plenty of fluids as directed by healthcare professionals, use fever-reducing medication at the correct dosage, and absolutely avoid self-medicating with aspirin, ibuprofen, or other non-steroidal anti-inflammatory drugs (NSAIDs) as these can increase the risk of bleeding. Besides early detection and timely treatment, experts recommend that people proactively prevent the disease by eliminating stagnant water containers, destroying mosquito larvae, using mosquito bite prevention measures, and consulting with healthcare professionals about dengue fever vaccination when appropriate. According to the Ministry of Health , in the first 10 months of 2026, the whole country recorded more than 170,000 cases of dengue fever, an increase of 26% compared to the same period, with 21 deaths. According to the Department of Disease Prevention (Ministry of Health), the South currently accounts for 66.8% of the total dengue fever cases nationwide, followed by the Central Highlands at 17.5%, the Central region at 9.8%, and the North at 5.9%. The disease tends to circulate year-round in many localities, with all four dengue virus types (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, DENV-4) appearing simultaneously. Of these, DENV-1 and DENV-2 are dominant, accounting for approximately 90% of samples; DENV-3 was re-recorded from late 2024 after a period of low detection. Recently, Dong Nai province recorded the deaths of two women, aged 38 and 59, due to dengue shock, one of whom had underlying diabetes. Both self-treated at home and were only hospitalized on the fourth day of their illness in a state of shock, with blood clotting disorders, liver and kidney damage, and damage to multiple organs. You may also like With dengue fever continuing to put pressure on the healthcare system, the availability of a vaccine in Vietnam is seen as a significant step forward, adding another tool to protect public health. Ms. Do Cao Van Anh, a senior advisor at Long Chau Pharmacy and Vaccination System, stated that the Dengue virus has four serotypes, meaning that individuals who have previously had dengue fever can be reinfected with a different serotype. Some subsequent infections may have a higher risk of severe complications due to antibody-dependent enhancement mechanisms. The availability of the Qdenga vaccine opens up more opportunities for proactive disease prevention, helping to reduce the risk of dengue fever and some of its serious consequences. However, the vaccine has only recently been introduced and is not yet part of the Expanded Immunization Program, while its reach and coverage in the community remain limited. According to Doctor Van Anh, the Qdenga vaccine is licensed in Vietnam for people aged 4 and older, with a two-dose schedule three months apart. Individuals who have or have not had dengue fever can be assessed for vaccination; prior testing for dengue infection is not mandatory. Notably, vaccination decisions must adhere to professional guidelines, especially for individuals with underlying health conditions or specific health issues. Individuals with acute illnesses and high fevers should postpone vaccination until their health stabilizes. For those who have recently recovered from dengue fever, the timing of vaccination should be advised by a doctor; some guidelines recommend waiting about six months after recovery. Women planning to become pregnant should complete vaccination at least one month before their expected pregnancy. 美属萨摩亚登革热暴发:CDC称更快的实验室检测有效但无法证明病例减少 - 技术时报

American Samoa Dengue Outbreak: CDC Credits Faster Lab Testing but Cannot Show Fewer Cases - Tech Times
新闻简报在美属萨摩亚,2025年6月至2026年8月期间发生了一次登革热暴发,导致1,048例确诊病例和371例住院,未报告死亡病例。CDC与美属萨摩亚卫生部门合作确认病例并实施控制措施,包括岛内检测,缩短了确诊时间。然而,报告并未提供证据表明更快的检测减少了暴发规模。该暴发的发病率是每10,000居民211例,10至14岁年龄段达到每10,000居民652例。大多数病例(95%)为本地感染,其中814名患者年龄在20岁以下,650名男性。美属萨摩亚唯一的医院LBJ医院在最繁忙的一天有18张床位被登革热患者占用,尽管估计有110张可用床位。应对措施包括对可到达的97%户外家庭和40%室内家庭进行喷洒,以及对医务人员进行培训和发布健康警报。尽管采取了这些措施,报告指出病例数和住院人数与2016至2018年的暴发情况非常接近,但由于缺乏对照组,无法确定更快检测对结果的影响。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该暴发发生在2025年6月至2026年8月之间,报告于2026年11月12日发布。暴发高峰出现在2025年12月,最繁忙的一天也处于该时期。
地点与对象
该暴发发生在美属萨摩亚,影响了76个村庄中的54个。人口中确诊了1,048例病例,其中814名患者(78%)年龄在20岁以下,650名(62%)为男性。
事件
美属萨摩亚报告了一次登革热暴发,2025年6月至2026年8月期间共有1,048例确诊病例和371例住院。CDC与美属萨摩亚卫生部门合作确认病例并实施控制策略。

该暴发的发病率是每10,000居民211例,10至14岁年龄段达到每10,000居民652例。美属萨摩亚唯一的医院LBJ医院在最繁忙的一天有18张床位被登革热患者占用,平均住院时间为四天。未报告死亡病例,报告中也未提及重症登革热或重症监护入院情况。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

American Samoa Dengue Outbreak: CDC Credits Faster Lab Testing but Cannot Show Fewer Cases - Tech Times

News Brief
In American Samoa, a dengue outbreak between June 2025 and August 2026 resulted in 1,048 confirmed cases and 371 hospitalizations, with no deaths reported. The CDC and American Samoa Department of Health collaborated to confirm cases and implement vector control measures, including on-island testing that reduced confirmation time. However, the report does not provide evidence that faster testing reduced the outbreak's scale. The incidence rate was 211 per 10,000 residents, peaking at 652 per 10,000 among ages 10 to 14. Most cases (95%) were locally acquired, with 814 patients under 20 and 650 male. LBJ Hospital, the territory's only hospital, faced strain with 18 beds occupied by dengue patients on its busiest day, though it had an estimated 110 usable beds. The outbreak response included spraying 97% of reachable households outdoors and 40% indoors, along with clinician training and health alerts. Despite these efforts, the report notes that the case count and hospitalization share closely match the 2016 to 2018 outbreak, but without a comparison group, it cannot determine the impact of faster testing on outcomes.
Original source text (English)
CDC's Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report (MMWR) said on November 12 that American Samoa's dengue outbreak produced 1,048 confirmed cases and 371 hospitalizations between June 2025 and August 2026 with no deaths, and that the territory's on-island laboratory confirmed its first cases within days instead of weeks. The report does not test whether that speed reduced cases, and its case count and hospitalization share closely match the territory's 2016 to 2018 outbreak, so residents and health officials still lack evidence that faster confirmation changed outcomes. CDC staff wrote the report with the American Samoa Department of Health (ASDOH). They call it the first dengue outbreak in the territory with the benefit of on-island confirmatory testing, and they conclude that such testing "can be considered as an effective strategy" to confirm infections and guide rapid local vector control. The incidence was 211 per 10,000 residents, based on 2020 census figures. Outbreak Grew From Six Cases to 1,048 Confirmed Across 54 of 76 Villages American Samoa recorded its first positive dengue test of 2025 on June 20. On July 8, the territory declared a public health emergency after six confirmed cases: two acquired locally and four tied to travel to neighboring Samoa, which was fighting a large outbreak of its own. Imported infections dominated early. Of 97 cases in June through August 2025, 38 (39%) were travel-associated. From September 2025 through February 2026, only 11 of 783 cases (1%) were, and no travel-associated case appeared after February 15, 2026. Overall, 999 cases were locally acquired (95%) and 49 (5%) were travel-associated. By subtraction, roughly 168 cases fall between March and August 2026, a figure the report does not state. Children and teens carried the burden: Age and sex. 814 patients (78%) were younger than 20. Incidence peaked at 652 per 10,000 in ages 10 to 14. 650 patients (62%) were male. 814 patients (78%) were younger than 20. Incidence peaked at 652 per 10,000 in ages 10 to 14. 650 patients (62%) were male. Geography. 54 of the territory's 76 villages (71%) reported at least one case. Tafuna had the most, with 152 cases (15%). 54 of the territory's 76 villages (71%) reported at least one case. Tafuna had the most, with 152 cases (15%). Hospital load. Weekly admissions peaked at 19 in December 2025, against a weekly median of 6. On the busiest day, dengue patients occupied 18 of about 110 usable beds (16%), and the median stay was four days. LBJ Hospital, the island's only hospital, was under construction for most of the outbreak. Its official capacity is 150 beds, but administrators reported about 110 were usable, an estimate rather than a formal count. How Do NS1 ELISA and RT-PCR Tests Differ for Dengue? Those two assays carried almost every confirmation in this outbreak. Of 1,048 confirmed cases, 838 (80%) were identified by NS1 ELISA and 204 (19%) by RT-PCR. Two cases were confirmed by IgM ELISA alone and four by a rapid test alone. The tests detect different things. An NS1 ELISA is a sandwich assay: a capture antibody fixed to a plastic plate pulls the dengue NS1 protein out of a serum sample, a second enzyme-linked antibody binds it, and a color change read by an instrument signals a positive. RT-PCR converts viral RNA into DNA and amplifies it, which detects the virus genome directly. CDC's clinical testing guidance matches each test to the day of illness. Molecular tests usually detect viral RNA in the first week, and NS1 antigen tests can be about as sensitive as molecular tests during days 0 to 7. IgM antibody testing can identify most recent infections starting around day 3, but positive IgM results can be affected by cross-reactivity with other flaviviruses such as Zika and West Nile. A negative RT-PCR or NS1 result does not rule out infection. The tradeoff matters here. An NS1 ELISA can confirm infection but cannot say which of the four serotypes caused it, and small studies suggest NS1 tests miss more cases in people who have been infected before. One Florida comparison of 21 serum samples found NS1 ELISA flagged 9 of 14 RT-PCR-positive samples, and the authors read the five it missed as likely non-primary infections. The MMWR counts confirmed cases only. It does not say how many people were tested or how many suspected cases tested negative, so readers cannot judge how many infections the NS1-heavy workflow missed. Serotyping Stayed Off-Island: Only 146 of 1,048 Cases Were Typed Only 146 cases (14%) were serotyped, at the Hawaii Department of Health laboratory using a CDC-developed RT-PCR assay. Of those, 136 (93%) were DENV-1 and 10 (7%) were DENV-2. No DENV-3 was reported. The territory's earlier outbreaks involved DENV-4 in 2009, DENV-3 in 2015 and DENV-2 in 2016 to 2018. Serotype is not a trivia item. Infection with one serotype gives lasting protection against that serotype but only short-term protection against the other three, and a later infection with a different serotype raises the risk of severe disease through a process called antibody-dependent enhancement. The MMWR does not discuss immunity or secondary infection, and it does not report severe dengue, intensive care admissions or IgG results. Case Count and Hospitalization Share Nearly Match 2016 to 2018 Outbreak The 2016 to 2018 DENV-2 outbreak produced 1,081 laboratory-confirmed cases over 24 months, and 380 of those patients (35.2%) were hospitalized. One patient died, transferred to American Samoa for care late in his illness. The 2025 to 2026 outbreak produced 1,048 confirmed cases between June 2025 and August 2026, with 371 hospitalizations (35%) and no deaths. The 2016 to 2018 workflow differed. Clinics screened suspected cases on the island with a rapid NS1 test, then sent specimens to Hawaii State Laboratories for RT-PCR confirmation. The 2025 report says the earlier outbreak prompted the on-island confirmatory testing, but it does not compare case counts or outcomes between the two outbreaks. A similar count does not prove the lab failed. The two outbreaks differ in length, in case definitions and in population denominators, and without a comparison group the report cannot say how large this outbreak would have grown with weeks-long confirmation. It does mean the headline numbers alone cannot show that faster testing reduced illness. Spraying Reached 97% of Reachable Households Outdoors but 40% Indoors The response combined cleanup, clinician training and insecticide: Alerts and training. ASDOH issued a dengue health alert in July 2025. In August 2025, CDC staff trained local clinicians in dengue management, and the hospital updated its surge plan to reallocate staff and space. In a CDC conference abstract, 93% of surveyed clinicians said their dengue knowledge increased after the training. ASDOH issued a dengue health alert in July 2025. In August 2025, CDC staff trained local clinicians in dengue management, and the hospital updated its surge plan to reallocate staff and space. In a CDC conference abstract, 93% of surveyed clinicians said their dengue knowledge increased after the training. Cleanups. The American Samoa Environmental Protection Agency ran community cleanups that removed about 250 large loads of household waste. The American Samoa Environmental Protection Agency ran community cleanups that removed about 250 large loads of household waste. Institutional spraying. ASDOH applied residual insecticide at 52 day care centers, 33 public schools, 13 private schools, 11 government offices, six community health centers, five churches, one private business and the hospital. ASDOH applied residual insecticide at 52 day care centers, 33 public schools, 13 private schools, 11 government offices, six community health centers, five churches, one private business and the hospital. Household spraying. Crews offered outdoor and indoor residual spraying within one to two days of a diagnosis, and to neighbors within 50 meters (164 feet). Among 740 households reached by phone as of June 15, 2026, 716 (97%) had received outdoor spraying and 297 (40%) had received indoor spraying. Crews offered outdoor and indoor residual spraying within one to two days of a diagnosis, and to neighbors within 50 meters (164 feet). Among 740 households reached by phone as of June 15, 2026, 716 (97%) had received outdoor spraying and 297 (40%) had received indoor spraying. Spatial emanators. From March 2026, households that declined indoor spraying were offered transfluthrin emanators with a stated use duration of one year. The program distributed 422 to 77 households, and 331 were installed in schools. Indoor spraying relied on deltamethrin and bifenthrin, both pyrethroids. Outdoor products included deltamethrin and combinations with the insect growth regulator pyriproxyfen. Crews treated water habitats with Bacillus thuringiensis israelensis and spinosad. Rapid Confirmation Only Helps If Control Tools Work, and Report Does Not Measure That The authors say on-island testing can guide rapid deployment of local vector control. The report does not evaluate that control. It describes no comparison between treated and untreated areas, no mosquito or case measurements for the emanators, and no resistance testing of local Aedes populations. Resistance has undermined pyrethroid spraying elsewhere. A randomized trial in Mérida, Mexico of indoor residual spraying found that deltamethrin produced no measurable reduction in adult Aedes aegypti where local mosquitoes were highly resistant. Researchers in Martinique found pyrethroid resistance undermined space spraying against dengue vectors. Those results show a risk, not a finding about American Samoa, because the report says nothing about local mosquitoes' susceptibility. The emanator evidence is also narrow. A placebo-controlled trial in Iquitos, Peru, covering 26 clusters and 2,907 households, found a transfluthrin emanator cut Aedes-borne virus infection by 34.1%. The same trial detected no statistical difference in clinical disease, with only 96 cases in total. Its authors cited imperfect household coverage and participant movement as factors that could dilute the effect, and noted the study was powered to detect a 30% reduction in infection risk, not in clinical disease. The Peru devices were placed at one product per 9 square meters (97 square feet) and replaced every 15 days. The MMWR describes American Samoa's product as a one-year use duration and does not say whether the two are the same. Territory's Prior-Infection Rate Cleared Vaccine Threshold, but No Dengue Vaccine Is Available Dengue is not new to the territory. A 2023 serosurvey of 887 students aged 7 to 16 estimated that 60% (95% confidence interval 48% to 72%) of those aged 9 to 16 had been infected before. CDC advises vaccine introduction only where at least 20% of age-eligible people have had dengue before, and the report states that American Samoa exceeds that threshold. That path has closed. Sanofi discontinued Dengvaxia in 2024, citing low global demand, and CDC says there are currently no dengue vaccines available in the United States. In a page dated October 26, 2026, the American Academy of Pediatrics said all available doses expired in fall 2026. Dengvaxia was a three-dose vaccine for children ages 9 to 16 with laboratory-confirmed prior infection, because its manufacturer cited the risk of worse outcomes in people never infected. Takeda's Qdenga is approved in other countries, but Takeda withdrew its U.S. application in 2023. The new MMWR report does not discuss vaccination. Weekly CDC Tables Count Calendar-Year Cases and Will Not Match Report CDC's National Notifiable Diseases Surveillance System (NNDSS) weekly tables list 417 provisional dengue cases for American Samoa in 2026 for the week ending September 26, against 191 at the same point in 2025. The week ending July 4 showed 353 cases in 2026 against 4 in 2025, and the MMWR says the territory's first positive test of 2025 came on June 20. Those counts are provisional and subject to change and are tallied by calendar year, while the MMWR covers June 2025 through August 2026, so the two series cannot be added or compared directly. A separate Pacific surveillance summary put the cumulative total at 1,036 laboratory-confirmed cases as of July 21, 2026, close to the report's 1,048 through August, though the counts may not use identical definitions. Report Is Preliminary: No Comparison Group, No Severity Data, Recalled Travel History The authors frame the report as a field note and include no formal limitations section. Several constraints are visible in the text: No comparison group, so the report cannot test whether any single intervention reduced transmission. Only 14% of cases were serotyped (146 of 1,048), so DENV-1 dominance may not describe the whole outbreak. Travel status depends on patients recalling trips in the 14 days before symptom onset. Spraying coverage comes only from 740 households reached by phone. Incidence uses 2020 census denominators, which may not match the current population. The report gives no end date for the public health emergency declaration, which a CDC conference abstract described as still active in mid-April 2026. The measurable result is speed: first cases confirmed within days, with 80% of confirmations resting on one on-island test. What the report leaves open is whether that speed changed the outcome. Residents and health officials weighing similar claims about local lab capacity can ask what outcome was compared, whether serotyping and insecticide-resistance testing are available on site, and whether the control tools the lab triggers have trial evidence behind them. Until vaccines return, CDC's long-standing advice is to prevent mosquito bites and control mosquitoes in and around the home. Frequently Asked Questions How do NS1 and PCR tests for dengue differ? NS1 tests look for a viral protein and can confirm infection in the acute phase, but they cannot name the serotype. RT-PCR looks for viral RNA, usually works in the first week and, as in Hawaii's lab for this outbreak, can identify the serotype. A negative result on either one does not rule out dengue. Did faster testing reduce dengue cases in American Samoa? The CDC report does not say. Its authors credit on-island testing with quicker confirmation and guidance for vector control, but it has no comparison group and does not compare case counts with the 2016 to 2018 outbreak, which had 1,081 confirmed cases and 380 hospitalizations. Is there a dengue vaccine available in the United States? CDC says no dengue vaccine is currently available in the United States after Sanofi discontinued Dengvaxia, and the American Academy of Pediatrics reports that remaining doses expired in fall 2026. Do spatial emanators prevent dengue? One placebo-controlled trial in Peru found a transfluthrin emanator reduced Aedes-borne virus infection by 34.1% but detected no statistical difference in clinical disease. The MMWR reports how many emanators American Samoa distributed but no outcome measures for them
View five news elements
Time
The outbreak occurred between June 2025 and August 2026, with the report published on November 12, 2026. The peak incidence was in December 2025, and the busiest day was during that period.
Location and population
The outbreak took place in American Samoa, affecting 54 of 76 villages. The population was 1,048 confirmed cases, with 814 patients (78%) under 20 years old and 650 (62%) male.
Event
An outbreak of dengue virus was reported in American Samoa, with 1,048 confirmed cases and 371 hospitalizations between June 2025 and August 2026. The CDC and American Samoa Department of Health collaborated to confirm cases and implement vector control strategies.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak had an incidence rate of 211 per 10,000 residents, peaking at 652 per 10,000 in ages 10 to 14. The territory's only hospital, LBJ Hospital, had 18 beds occupied by dengue patients on its busiest day, with a median stay of four days. No deaths were reported, and the report does not mention severe dengue or intensive care admissions.
Response, status and uncertainty
The CDC credits faster on-island lab testing as an effective strategy for confirming infections and guiding rapid vector control. However, the report does not provide evidence that this speed reduced the number of cases. The outbreak response included spraying 97% of reachable households outdoors and 40% indoors, along with clinician training and health alerts. The report notes that the case count and hospitalization share closely match the 2016 to 2018 outbreak, but without a comparison group, it cannot determine the impact of faster testing.
